Let's derive LP approach for offpeak.
Given current a>=0, scale arbitrary. Let P = max_off(a*a). We seek direction g>=0 to decrease P after blend: a_t = (1-t)a + t g (rescale sum). For small t, directional derivative of max_off? We want choose g to minimize increase of upper envelope. Standard LP: maximize sum(g) (or match normalization) subject to cross offpeaks (a*g)_k <= P for all off k, and maybe g nonnegative. Center unconstrained because objective ignores it. Then rescale g to sum = sum a. Blend. This should not increase max a*g off beyond P, and decreases a*a? We need compare. The new offpeak <= (1-t)P + tP = P? Actually for a_t*a_t off = (1-t)^2(a*a)+(2t(1-t))(a*g)+t^2(g*g). We don't constrain g*g. For small t and g comparable, a*g <=P ensures first-order <=P, but g*g may have large peaks causing increase for non-small t. Use small t or constrain g*g? The previous LP only f*g <=M, not g*g. Then for small t score nonincreasing? They used M = ||f*f||∞ (maybe off) and g rescaled. For full center, includes. For off, (g*g) term could increase; but small t helps. We can use line search over t to ensure improvement. LP g maximizes sum under a*g<=P, so it tends to put mass where a*g lags low, potentially g*g off can be high. We can constrain also g*g <= c P? That's quadratic non-LP. Could instead after LP blend small.

Alternative: Use smooth gradient max approximation. For offpeak set K = lags 1..n-1. Conv off = C(a,a). We can minimize m_t(C_off)=logsumexp((C_off-P?)/t) maybe. Gradient w.r.t a: subgradient = sum_l w_l * shifted a_l where w_l proportional to exp(C_l/t). If using soft-max of positive offpeaks, gradient = 2 * convolution of w with reversed a over relevant? Let's derive.
C_k = sum_i a_i a_{i+k}, for k=0..n-1 (linear). For k>0. Gradient w.r.t a_j: dC_k/da_j = a_{j+k} if j+k<n plus a_{j-k} if j-k>=0? For k positive. More generally d/d a of sum_k w_k C_k = 2 * crosscorr of v with a, where v_k = w_k? For k>=0, C_k = sum_{i=0}^{n-1-k} a_i a_{i+k}. Key properties:
- Score invariant to scale; fix sum S=sqrt(2n) to make objective max coefficient.
- Nonnegative constraints. Softmax parameterization ensures positive, fixed sum.
- Objective `max_k C_k` where C = conv(a,a). The feasible set is nonconvex (quadratic). But smooth max approximation + Adam can converge.
- LP method: For current a, M = max C. We need find direction g (nonnegative) that when added reduces max C. LP max sum(g) s.t. a*g <= M, g>=0 (and maybe constraints). This is like finding a vector in the polar? For small t, if a*g <= M and g*g not too high, C_{a_t} <=? C_{a+tg} = C_a + 2t C_{a,g} + t^2 C_g. If C_{a,g}<=M and C_a<=M, first order increases by at most? We want decrease, but a itself feasible. Maximizing sum(g) may find g other than a. New C may lower some peaks due to redistribution, but t^2 C_g could increase. Use line search.

LP solver:
```
def lp_best_response(a, S, max_time=5.0):
    n=len(a)
    A = np.zeros((2*n-1, n))
    # build by loops or vectorized
    for k in range(2*n-1):
        # g_j coefficient a[k-j] if 0<=k-j<n
        start = max(0, k-(n-1))
        end = min(n-1, k)
        j_indices = np.arange(start, end+1)
        A[k, j_indices] = a[k-j_indices]
```
O(n^2) fill. For n=256, fine. Can optimize by diagonal pattern: For each j, column j has a at rows j..j+n-1. Use loop j: A[j:j+n,j]=a. This is same as previous. 256 loops of 256 assignments fast.
Add t column: `A_full = np.empty((2*n-1, n+1)); A_full[:, :n]=A; A_full[:, n] = -1.0`
b_ub zeros(2n-1)
A_eq = [ones(n),0]
c_obj zeros(n+1); c_obj[n]=1.
bounds: [(0,S) for n] + [(0,None)]
Use `linprog(..., method='highs', options={'presolve': True, 'time_limit': max_time})`
Return g,t.
```
Potential infeasibility if a has all zeros. We'll avoid.
```

### LP best-response (min cross peak)
```
def lp_best_response(a, S, time_limit=10.0):
    n = len(a)
    A = np.zeros((2*n-1, n+1))
    for j in range(n):
        A[j:j+n, j] = a
    A[:, n] = -1.0
    c = np.zeros(n+1); c[n]=1.0
    b_ub = np.zeros(2*n-1)
    A_eq = np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1
    b_eq = np.array([S])
    bounds = [(0,S)]*n + [(0,10*S)] # upper t
    res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve': True, 'time_limit': time_limit})
    if res.success:
       g=res.x[:n]; t=res.x[n]; g=np.clip(g,0,S); return g,t
    # fallback: return a, current max
```
If `hiops` time limit exceeded but has feasible solution? `res.x` maybe not. We can check `res.status`? Use `if res.success or (res.x is not None and np.allclose(A_eq@res.x,b_eq))`? Simpler if success.
Upper bound t = maybe 10*S or current score*2. But t is coefficient value (since sum S), uniform t=2. Set upper = 10*S? huge. Use `best_score` maybe 2*S? Since score max coefficient around score. For S~22, scores ~1-2; t<=10 enough. But for bad initial S^2? uniform 2. Random may have max conv up to S^2 if spike, t huge. Set upper=10*S (220) safe. Or no upper. In linprog, variable t can be unbounded if a=0, but a positive. bounds t=(0,None).
```

```

### LP fixed-point routine
```
def lp_refine(a_init, time_limit, max_iters=200, verbose=False):
    n=len(a_init); S=np.sqrt(2*n); a=rescale(a_init,S)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score_fast(a)
    t0=time.monotonic()
    stagnant=0; eta=0.25
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time>limit: break
       g,tmin = lp_best_response(a,S,time_limit=8)
       if g is None: break
       g = rescale(g,S)
       # evaluate g and blends
       cand = None; cand_score=best_score
       for eta in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0]:
           x=(1-eta)*a+eta*g
           x=rescale(x,S)
           sc=score_fast(x)
           if sc<cand_score:
               cand_score=sc; cand=x.copy()
       if cand is not None:
           a=cand; best=a.copy(); best_score=cand_score; stagnant=0; eta maybe increase
       else:
           stagnant +=1
           if stagnant > 5: # perturb
              a = rescale(a + 0.05*rng.standard_normal(n), S)
              stagnant=0
              eta=0.1
       if verbose: print
    return best,best_score
```
But re-solving LP from `a` each iteration. Maybe use `a` current, not best? If improvement, keep. If no, perturb.

Could solve LP with a target `M` rather than min t to speed? The min t LP has objective t; may be slightly harder but okay. It returns tmin. If tmin is much lower than score, direction good.

Potential problem: The LP best response `g` might be exactly uniform or similar, and score(g) not better. The blend line search may not improve. Need gradient polish.

### Gradient polish improved
We can implement a robust soft-max Adam with adaptive tau and multiple restarts.

Parameter choices:
- We want minimize exact max. Use log-sum-exp smooth. The optimum of smooth approx may differ; use small tau at end.
- We can run a schedule with phases:
   1. Coarse: tau = 20/n? For n=256, 0.078; weights broad; many Adam steps (e.g. 2000) to get out of local.
   2. Medium: tau = 4/n (0.0156); 5000 steps.
   3. Fine: tau = 0.5/n (0.002); 10000 steps.
   4. Very fine: tau = 0.1/n (0.0004); maybe 20000 if time.
But previous tau_end 0.3/n =0.001. Seems fine.
- Adam lr: Since gradient of soft objective (no tau) magnitude? c values ~1.5, derivative g ~ sum w*a? Could be O(1). Learning rate for theta: need small. Previous base_lr=0.8 with decay. Maybe okay. We can adapt with backtracking: if score increases, reduce lr; if improves, increase slightly.
- Use `theta` initialized from current `a`: `theta=np.log(np.clip(a,1e-15,None)/S)`? Actually softmax(theta)*S=a. So theta=log(a/S) (if a positive). Subtract max for stability.
- At each step:
   a=softmax(theta)*S.
   c=conv_square(a) (FFT)
   cmax=c.max()
   z=(c-cmax)/tau
   w=exp(z)/sum(exp(z))
   g=grad from w (FFT)
   g_theta = a*g - (a/S)*dot(a,g)
   Adam update.
   Compute new a, exact score; update best.
- To avoid numerator overflow, use `zmax` =0 after subtract cmax. `exp(z)` fine (≤1). sum ≥1.
- For very small tau, only max coefficient weight; gradient hard. If multiple equal max, weights equal. Good.
- We can also add a small entropy regularization? Softmax already.

Potential issue: The exact score may not monotonically decrease with smooth objective. We can choose step size via line search on theta? Too expensive. Use Adam with decay and revert if bad:
   Store last best theta. If current exact score > best + delta for many steps, restart from best theta with smaller tau/lr or perturb.
- Use `momentum` adaptive.

Maybe use a *direct multiplicative update* for better stability:
For soft weights w, gradient G positive? Since a,g nonnegative, G=2 convolution of reversed a with nonnegative w, all nonnegative. g_theta = a(G - mean_aG). This drives high G_j down, low up, preserving sum. Multiplicative update `a_j *= exp(-η G_j / S?)` with renormalize. This is similar to mirror descent on entropy. Could be more stable than Adam. We can implement both.

Mirror descent update for softmax theta: theta -= lr * g_theta. Equivalent to multiplicative with exponential. Adam maybe better.

Let c_sorted descending. If top few coefficients close, tau should be comparable to their spacing times log K? We want weights proportional to importance of reducing max. If gap = c0-c1. ### Direct peak shaving via subgradient and line search
At exact optimum, subgradient of max is any convex combination of gradients of active max coefficients. We can compute `w` as one-hot on max coefficient (or uniform over max). Use gradient descent with small step. This could fine-tune after smooth anneal. Procedure:
- Find all coefficients with c_k > cmax - eps (e.g. 1e-3). Use uniform weights over them.
- Compute gradient G (FFT as above). Update theta with small lr (e.g. 0.01-0.1) and line search over factors; keep best.
- Repeat. This directly lowers current peaks.
Could get stuck due to non-smooth but good final.

Let's define efficient FFT convolution:
```
def fft_convolve(a,b=None):
    if b is None: b=a
    n=len(a); m=len(b)
    size=1
    while size < n+m-1:
        size <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    if b is a:
        fb=fa.copy()? Actually rfft(a) again; can square.
    else: fb=np.fft.rfft(b,size)
    out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,size)
    return out[:n+m-1]
```
For square: `fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size); out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,size)`. Avoid copy? `fa*fa`.
Need handle sizes: for gradient w length 2n-1, conv rev_a length n -> size >=3n-2. Use function.

`conv_square(a)`:
```
n=len(a); size=1; while size<2*n-1: size<<=1
fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
return np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,size)[:2*n-1]
```
For n=1, size=1; conv length1.

```
s=a.sum()
if s <= 1e-300: return np.ones(n)/n*S
return a * (S/s)
```

`softmax(theta,S)`:
```
z=theta - theta.max()
e=np.exp(z)
p=e/e.sum()
return p*S
```

### LP building efficiency

def build_cross_matrix(a):
    n=len(a)
    A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n))
    for j in range(n):
        A[j:j+n,j]=a
    return A
```

### Gradient polish implementation details
Let's write `gradient_polish(a_init, time_limit, tau_init=None, tau_end=None, base_lr=0.5, verbose=False, rng=None)`:
```
n=len(a_init); S=np.sqrt(2*n)
a0=rescale(a_init,S)
theta=np.log(np.clip(a0,1e-300,None)/S)
# center theta
theta=theta - theta.max()
best=a0.copy(); best_score=score_fast(a0)
m1=np.zeros(n); m2=np.zeros(n); beta1=0.9; beta2=0.999; eps=1e-8; step=0
t0=time.monotonic()
# schedule phases based on elapsed fraction
# use dynamic tau: tau = tau_start * (tau_end/tau_start)**frac
# but maybe allow tau overrides
lr=base_lr
stall=0
while time < limit:
   frac=min(1,(time-t0)/time_limit)
   tau = tau_start * (tau_end/tau_start)**frac
   a=softmax(theta,S)
   c=conv_square(a)
   cmax=c.max()
   z=(c-cmax)/tau
   # to avoid underflow, exp(z) where z<=0, sum>=1
   e=np.exp(z)
   w=e/e.sum()
   g=grad_from_weights(a,w) # returns length n
   inner=np.dot(a*(g))? Actually sum_j G_j a_j
   g_theta = a*g - (a/S)*inner
   # Adam
   step+=1
   m1=beta1*m1+(1-beta1)*g_theta
   m2=beta2*m2+(1-beta2)*(g_theta*g_theta)
   m1h=m1/(1-beta1**step); m2h=m2/(1-beta2**step)
   # adapt lr: maybe base_lr / sqrt(1+0.01*step)
   lr = base_lr / np.sqrt(1.0 + 0.02*step)
   theta_new = theta - lr * m1h/(np.sqrt(m2h)+eps)
   a_new=softmax(theta_new,S)
   score=score_fast(a_new)
   if np.isfinite(score):
       if score < best_score:
           best_score=score; best=a_new.copy(); theta=theta_new; stall=0
       else:
           # accept if not too bad? To avoid local, accept small increases
           if score < best_score + 0.005: # or current?
               theta=theta_new
               stall +=1
           else:
               # revert, reduce lr?
               theta = theta # no update? But momentum already updated. Could keep theta, reset current? 
               stall +=1
           if stall > 50:
               theta = best_logcopy? 
               # perturb
               if rng is not None:
                   theta = theta + 0.01*rng.standard_normal(n)
               stall=0
               base_lr *= 0.7 (if we store mutable)
   # occasional evaluate true? not inside gradient to save time, final.
```
Need be careful: If we don't accept theta, momentum was updated with gradient from old a. It's okay but can drift; we can keep `theta` unchanged but momentum updated? Usually update momentum regardless? Maybe simpler: always accept theta_new (score may increase) but keep best; if score worsens too much, revert to last best and reset lr. This is like stochastic gradient with anneal. Since smooth objective may increase exact score. We can use line search factor: try theta_new, if score > current_score*(1+tol) scale step down. But computing score each step. Let's implement current tracking:
- Store `current_a`, `current_score` for accepted trajectory.
- For each proposed, if score <= current_score: accept. Else if score < current_score + 0.001: accept (small uphill). Else try smaller step: `theta_try = theta - 0.5*lr*...` maybe 2/4. If still bad, revert and reduce lr.
But `m1/m2` update each attempt could be costly. Simpler: Adam with small lr likely okay. We can always accept to follow smooth objective, but keep best exact. If it wanders too far/exact score much worse, restart from best with higher tau/lower lr.

We can maintain `best_theta` when best updated.

To improve, use *adaptive step size* via backtracking on exact score:

for attempt in range(3):

### Peak shaving final
After gradient, exact score maybe best. We can do subgradient on active peaks:
```
def peak_shave(a_init, time_limit, tau_min=0.0, rng=None):
   a=rescale(a_init,S); best...
   theta=log(a/S)
   m1/m2 Adam maybe
   for step:

      w = zero; w[active]=1/len(active)
      g=grad_from_weights(a,w)

      score...

```
This is like gradient with tau→0. It may oscillate among peaks; averaging active helps. Use Adam and best.

### Multi-start initializations
Let's define `make_inits(n, rng, provided=None)`:
- uniform: ones.
- reverse sym? Uniform.
- provided resampled to n.
- chirp: `1 + alpha*cos(pi*i*i/n) + noise`, positive.
- bathtub: `1 - alpha*cos(2π*i/(n-1))`? Cos low at center? For i=0 center? Let's index: cos(0)=1 at edges if use `(i - (n-1)/2)^2`? Bathtub high edges low center: `1 - alpha*cos(2π*i/(n-1))` gives i=0 cos=1 =>0 (low edge) not. Use `1 + alpha*cos(2π * (i - (n-1)/2)/(n-1))`? At center cos=1, edges cos=-? Actually if phase = π*i/(n-1), at center i=n/2 phase=π/2 cos0; not. Simpler: distance to edges normalized: d = min(i, n-1-i)/(n/2). Bathtub = 1 + alpha*abs(d-0.5)*2? High edges, low center: `1 + alpha*np.abs(0.5 - d)`? At edges d=0 or1, value 1+0.5α; center 1. Or `1 + alpha*np.cos(np.pi*d)`? At d=0/1 cos=π -1, center cos0=1 -> low edges high center. Use `1 - alpha*np.cos(np.pi*d)` gives edges 2-α, center1. Good.
- two-lobe: Gaussian at quarters: `exp(-((i-n/4)^2)/(2σ^2)) + exp(-((i-3n/4)^2)/...)`.
- edge spike: `1 + beta*exp(-((i)^2/(2σ^2))) + beta*exp(-(((n-1-i))^2)/...)`.
- random: 1+spread*N(0,1), clipped.
- smoothed noise: use FFT random phases, generate smooth nonnegative.
- deterministic sinusoidal: `1 + α cos(2π k i/n + φ)` for k=1,2,3.
We can randomly choose parameters.

But starting from provided is likely best. We can also use sequences generated by a quick LP from uniform.

### Length search and interpolation
For a candidate a length m, to resize to n:
- If m==n return.
- Use `np.interp(np.linspace(0,1,n), np.linspace(0,1,m), a)` (linear). This preserves sum roughly. Then rescale.
Could also zero-pad? Maybe not.

- Phase LP if use_lp and n<=1024 and time_limit>30:
   lp_budget = min(0.45*time_limit, 200.0)
   run `lp_refine(a, lp_budget, rng)`
- Phase gradient:
   grad_budget = min(0.45*time_limit, 400.0)
   if time remaining: run `gradient_polish(best, grad_budget, rng)`
- Phase peak shave:
   peak_budget = min(0.2*time_limit, 100.0)
   run `gradient_polish` with small tau (or `peak_shave`)
- Phase SA if time >30:
   run `sa_polish`
- return best.

### Tune LP refine
Maybe the LP min t best response can be run many times. Let's design an efficient loop:
```
def lp_refine(a_init, time_limit, rng=None, max_iters=100, lp_time=5.0, verbose=False):
   n=len(a); S=...
   a=rescale(a_init,S); best=a; best_score=score_fast(a)
   t0=time.monotonic(); iter=0; no_improve=0; eta_base=0.25
   while time_limit and iter<max_iters:
      g,tmin=lp_best_response(a,S,lp_time=time.min(10,time_left/10?))
      if g is None: break
      g=rescale(g,S); score_g=score_fast(g)
      # try blends
      best_cand=None; best_cscore=best_score
      for eta in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.75,1.0):
          x=(1-eta)*a+eta*g; x=rescale(x,S); sc=score_fast(x)
          if sc<best_cscore: best_cscore=sc; best_cand=x.copy()
      if best_cand is not None:
          a=best_cand; best=a.copy(); best_score=best_cscore; no_improve=0
          # maybe increase eta? not needed because line search per iteration
      else:
          no_improve +=1
          if no_improve > 3:
              # perturb current
              if rng is not None:
                 a=rescale(a+0.03*rng.standard_normal(n),S)
              no_improve=0
          if no_improve > 15: break
      iter+=1
      if verbose: print
   return best,best_score
```
But if `score_g` is already good, blend eta=1 includes. Good.
LP time: If lp_time 5s, 100 iter=500s. maybe too much. max 30. Use `lp_time = 3.0` for n=256, maybe 6 for 512. We can adapt.
Could solve LP from `best` instead of current `a`? If current improved, solve from best for stability. But best=current usually.
Potential LP solution g may have sum S after rescale even if LP sum=S exactly; rescale no change.

Let's design `subgradient_polish(a_init, time_limit, rng=None)`:
- Softmax theta from a.
- Use Adam but weights uniform over active peaks within tolerance `eps_active`.
- Adaptive eps: start 0.02, decrease to 1e-4.
- Also randomize among top K (e.g., K=3) to avoid cycling.
- Learning rate smaller.

Pseudo:
```
def peak_shave_polish(a_init, time_limit, rng=None, base_lr=0.1):
   a=rescale; theta=log(a/S)
   best...
   m1,m2...
   t0=time.monotonic(); step=0
   eps_start=0.02; eps_end=0.0001
   active_fraction_start=5? 
   while time:
      frac=...
      eps=...
      a=softmax(theta,S)
      c=conv_square(a); cmax=c.max()
      # active
      tol=eps*max(1,cmax)
      active=np.where(c>=cmax-tol)[0]
      # maybe top 16 by value
      if len(active)>16: active= c.argsort()[-16:]
      w=np.zeros(len(c)); w[active]=1.0/len(active)
      g=grad_from_weights(a,w)
      g_theta=...
      Adam update
      score...
      if best improve update
   return best
```
This is similar to `gradient_polish` with dynamic weights; we can generalize `gradient_polish(a_init,time_limit,weight_mode='softmax'|'peak', tau_params...)`. But separate okay.

### SA polish implementation
Let's design a fast SA using local score. For n up to 1024, score via FFT each iteration. We can do many (thousands). Score differences small; temperature adaptive.
```
def sa_polish(a_init, time_limit, rng):
    n; S; a=rescale; best=a; best_score=score_fast(a); current=a; current_score=best_score
    t0=time.monotonic(); temp=0.001; cool=0.9995; iter=0
    while time:
        cand=a.copy()
        r=rng.random()
        if r<0.5:
            c=conv_square(a); k=int(np.argmax(c))
            # contributions to c_k: i and k-i valid
            contrib=np.zeros(n)
            # vectorized: i range max(0,k-n+1)..min(n-1,k); contrib[i]=a[i]*a[k-i]
            lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
            contrib[lo:hi+1] = a[lo:hi+1]*a[k-lo:k-hi+1? Need careful]
```
For c_k = sum_i a_i a_{k-i}. For i in [lo,hi], k-i in [k-hi,k-lo] valid. We can compute pairs `i=lo..hi`, `j=k-i` decreasing. In numpy:
```
i = np.arange(lo, hi+1)
j = k - i
contrib[i] = a[i]*a[j]
```
Then choose top m positions to decrease and others increase. But convolve coefficient c_k may correspond to left side k<n-1 or right side >n-1. We can just use k as index 0..2n-2; formula works.
Decrease top contributors: multiply by exp(normal( -0.02,0.01)). Increase random non-top: exp(+0.02). Renormalize.
   elif r<0.75: random segment multiplicative.
   elif r<0.9: two segments.
   else: global Gaussian multiplicative small.
        cand = np.clip(cand,0,None); rescale; new_score=score_fast(cand)
        if new_score < current_score: accept; if <best update
        else if rng.random() < exp(-(new_score-current_score)/temp): accept
        temp *= cool; if temp <1e-6: temp=1e-5? 
```
Score target differences ~0.001, temp 1e-4 maybe. We can set temp initial based on 0.001 and decrease slowly. But if no improvements, very slow. We can also use `pool` restart.
SA may not be needed.

### Potential further mathematical improvement
Maybe use a *dual weighting* LP: The LP best response min max a*g can be solved; its dual gives weights w over conv coefficients such that for the optimal g, g_i ∝? Let's derive to maybe construct a fixed point directly.
Primal: min t
s.t. ∑_j A_{k,j} g_j ≤ t (k=0..2n-2)
∑_j g_j = S
g_j ≥0
t free ≥0.
Dual: variables λ_k ≥0 for A g ≤ t, and ν free for equality.
L = t + ∑ λ_k(A_k g - t) + ν(S - sum g) = ∑_j (A^T λ - ν) g_j + t(1 - ∑λ) + νS.
Inf over g≥0 finite if A^T λ - ν = 0 and? Inf over t≥0 finite if 1-∑λ =0 (t unbounded? t≥0: if coefficient <0, inf -∞? Need 1-∑λ=0). So dual maximize ν S subject to A^T λ = ν 1, ∑λ=1, λ≥0. Since ∑λ=1, ν =? This is find a probability distribution λ over coefficients such that A^T λ constant. Then ν = constant. This is independent of g. For a fixed a, ν = constant = ∑? The A matrix has entries a_{k-j}. A^T λ at column j = ∑_k λ_k a_{k-j} (with valid). Need constant ν. This is exactly a stationary distribution λ making expected weight over lags flat. If exists, the primal value t* = ν S? (since objective min t = νS). This is interesting. If we can find λ, the best response g has positive where dual slack. The sequence a is optimal if there exists λ probability over coefficients such that A(a)^T λ is constant and a itself is in argmin? Maybe KKT: At optimum a (of max a*a), there exist λ probabilities such that derivative? For objective max c, KKT at optimum maybe there is λ over active coefficients with gradient 2*constant? Since sum fixed, gradient of max can be constant. This suggests using dual weights.

We can use this for *direct iterative algorithm for a*: Given a, compute λ as a distribution over coefficients that makes weighted cross flat? But that's LP dual. However we can approximate λ by softmax of current c? Not.

The LP primal min t can also be seen as finding λ. If we use λ from current c (softmax), then gradient? That's the soft-max gradient! Indeed gradient with weights λ gives derivative direction; if λ makes A^T λ constant, then a is a stationary point. So gradient with softmax weights is attempting to find same λ. This is good.

Maybe we can optimize a by enforcing that for the current λ (max weights), the vector `A^T λ` is as constant as possible. The gradient of λ's objective? Hmm.

Could use a fixed point: Choose λ softmax of c(a) with temperature; update a to minimize variance of A^T λ under sum fixed, maybe via projection. Since G=2 A^T? Actually derivative G=2*conv(rev a, λ) relates to A^T λ? For coefficient weights λ, derivative = 2*(some) =? It equals 2*(a * λ) in Hankel form. Constant G would mean no gradient. We can update a toward making G flat. The softmax gradient descent does that.

Could use Newton to minimize variance of G? Not.

def gradient_polish(a_init, time_limit, rng=None, tau_start=None, tau_end=None, base_lr=None, verbose=False):
    n=len(a_init); S=np.sqrt(2*n)

    m1,m2=zeros; step=0

       frac=...
       tau=tau_start * (tau_end/tau_start)**frac
       a=softmax(theta,S)
       # compute c, score, weights
       c=conv_square(a); score=score_fast? We can score from c, S (since a sum S) but use function.
       cmax=c.max(); z=(c-cmax)/tau; e=exp(z); w=e/e.sum()
       g=grad_from_weights(a,w)
       inner=np.dot(a,g)
       gt=a*g - (a/S)*inner
       # maybe normalize gt by n? To make lr length independent. Previous used no. We can scale `gt /= n`? For large n, sum gt zero; magnitude? a~S/n. G~? If weights broad, G ~ 2∑_m w_{j+m}a_m. ### Gradient from weights implementation
```
def grad_from_weights(a,w):
    n=len(a)
    rev=a[::-1]
    conv=fft_convolve(rev,w)
    return 2.0*conv[n-1:n-1+n]
```
Check slice: For n=1, rev length1,w length1, conv length1, n-1=0 to1 ok. For n=3, conv rev length5, slice 2:5 length3. Good.

### Score from convex
Since we fix S, score = c.max() if sum=S. But due rescale, use `score_fast` which recomputes sum. We can write `score_from_conv(c,n,sum)` for speed.
```
def score_from_c(c,n,sum_a): return 2*n*max(c)/(sum_a*sum_a)
```
In gradient, a sum is S; we can use `c.max()`. But after softmax, sum exactly S within rounding. Use `c_max = float(c.max())`; score = c_max (since S^2=2n)? Wait if sum=S=sqrt(2n), score = 2n*c_max/S^2 = c_max. Yes. So exact score under sum fixed equals max coefficient. Great. We can use `score = cmax` (no division). But due rounding sum not exactly sqrt, close. Use cmax for speed. For candidates not sum fixed, use score_fast.
In `gradient_polish`, all a from softmax sum S; use `cmax` as score. But if S not exactly sqrt due rounding, okay.
For final local score, use score_fast.

This simplifies: target score ~1.5 means cmax ~1.5.

### LP under sum fixed
In LP, S fixed. The cross coefficients a*g have average S^2/(2n-1) ~1.002. Min t likely around 1.1-1.5. The objective g score if g fixed sum is c_gmax. Good.

### One-hot scoring under full conv
Onehot length n: a[0]=S. conv [S^2,0,...]? coefficient 0=S^2=2n. score=2n*S^2/S^2=2n (large). Uniform score 2. So better to optimize.

### Edge cases
- If n=1, conv length1, score=2. Not good. We can ignore n<16.
- Provided sequence may have zeros; gradient log needs clip. Use small floor `1e-300` but softmax can approach zeros. For stability, maybe use a *softplus parameterization*? Softmax fine.

To have them near average, a_0 ~ sqrt(avg) ~ S/√(2n) ~1 (since S=√(2n), avg≈1, a0≈1). Thus edge values not too small. In uniform with S=22.6, a_i=0.088, edge coefficient 0.0078 < avg 1; wait with sum fixed S, avg coeff ~1, uniform edge coeff = a_0^2 = 2/n? =0.0078, much below avg; center coeff=2 above avg. So the polynomial square is triangular: low edges, high center. To flatten, we need increase edges a0^2 to ~1, i.e. a0≈1, while maintaining sum S≈22.6 over 256 terms average 0.088. That requires huge contrast: edges ~1 (11x average), center maybe adjusted. Edge coefficient a0^2 should be ~1, so a0≈1. Since sum only 22.6, we can have at most ~22 entries of size1. This suggests sparse high-amplitude values at strategic positions. This makes sense: to flatten square coefficients, need high values at edges so low edge coefficients not too low, but not too many to keep sum. The polynomial square average ~1; each coefficient is sum of products. If a_i are around 1 for ~22 positions, c coefficients near counts of pairs. This becomes combinatorial: choose about 22 high weights such that pair sums are balanced.

This explains trivial one-hot bad (edge coeff S^2 huge). Need spread high values near edges with small values elsewhere? Edge coefficient a_0^2 directly sets first coefficient. To have max near 1.5, a_0 can be up to sqrt(1.5)≈1.225 if sum normalized? Actually under sum S, score=max coeff; edge coeff a0^2 ≤M. So a0≤√1.5≈1.225. With S≈22.6, we can have ~18 such peaks. A sequence of 18 ones would sum 18, below S; need extra small mass. Convolution of 18 spikes each height1 has coefficients equal to number of ordered pairs with given sum times h^2. Need max pair sum count ≤1.5? If h=1, count must ≤1.5, impossible if many pairs share sums? Need avoid multiple pairs same sum: choose set of positions such that all sums unique (Sidon set). For 18 positives in [0,255], maximum Sidon set size ~√n≈16; can have ~16 with all pair sums unique (ordered gives 2 count for distinct). If sums unique, coefficients at pair sums =2 (ordered) for distinct spikes, >1.5. Heights can be smaller to reduce. If we choose ~√n spikes with height h, sum ~k h, S≈22.6. Max coefficient from distinct pair ordered 2h^2 ≤1.5 => h≤0.866. Need k≥26 to sum 22.6, but Sidon max ~16. So need varied heights and maybe small background to fill sum without creating peaks. This is a nontrivial combinatorial/continuous design.

The LP/gradient can find such.

Then scale to sum: if k=16, h=22.6/16=1.41, max distinct pair coeff 2h^2=4 >1.5. Need spread mass over more positions with smaller weights, allowing multiple pair sums. This is like designing a positive measure on interval with flat convolution, akin to continuous version: a uniform density of height? Continuous nonnegative with finite support: convolution is triangular, peak. To make conv flat, need density not uniform? But continuous convolution of L1 positive has peak at interior by positivity? For continuous, if f≥0 on [0,L], convolution at L/2 integrates product over half interval; maximum likely near center? It can be flattened by a density that is high at edges and low center (bathtub). If a(s)=α+β cos(2πs) (nonnegative), convolution = α^2 * (1-|t-0.5|?) + β terms. Could flatten. Discrete similar. We can parametrize a_i = exp(θ * cos(π(i/(n-1)))?) high edges? Let's implement `fft_convolve` robust:
```
def fft_convolve(a,b=None):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if b is None: b=a
    else: b=np.asarray(b,dtype=np.float64)
    need = len(a)+len(b)-1
    size=1
    while size<need: size <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    if b is a: fb=None? Can't compare if asarray copies? Use a flag.
```
Simpler always compute both; for square use separate `conv_square`.
`conv_square`:
```
def conv_square(a):
    n=len(a); size=1; while size<2*n-1: size<<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,size)
    return c[:2*n-1]
```
For gradient, `fft_convolve(rev_a,w)` need length n + (2n-1)-1 =3n-2. Use generic:
```
def fft_convolve2(a,b):
   need=len(a)+len(b)-1; size=1; while size<need...
   return np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(a,size)*np.fft.rfft(b,size), size)[:need]
```
Could optimize by if b is same? not necessary.

`score_fast`:
```
def score_fast(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if s<0.01: return np.inf
    n=a.size
    c=conv_square(a)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    return float(2.0*n*m/(s*s))
```
This matches evaluator. For speed in inner loops, use `score_from_cmax(cmax,n,s)` maybe.

`rescale`:
```
def rescale(a,S):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-300:
       return np.full(a.shape, S/len(a))
    return a*(S/s)
```

`softmax`:
```
def softmax_logits(theta,S):
    z=theta - float(np.max(theta))
    e=np.exp(z)
    p=e/float(np.sum(e))
    return p*S
```
Need theta finite; if not, reset.

`grad_from_weights`:
```
def grad_from_weights(a,w):
    n=len(a)
    rev=a[::-1]
    conv=fft_convolve2(rev,w)
    return 2.0*conv[n-1:n-1+n]
```
If n=1 works.

### LP best response implementation details
```
def lp_best_response(a,S,time_limit=5.0):
    n=len(a)
    if n==0: return None,np.inf
    rows=2*n-1
    A=np.zeros((rows,n+1), dtype=np.float64)
    A[:,n] = -1.0
    # fill columns
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(n):
        A[j:j+n, j] = a
    c=np.zeros(n+1); c[n]=1.0
    b_ub=np.zeros(rows)
    A_eq=np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1.0
    b_eq=np.array([S])
    bounds=[(0.0,S)]*n + [(0.0,10.0*S)] # upper t
    try:
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve':True,'time_limit':float(time_limit)})
       if res.success or (res.x is not None and len(res.x)==n+1):
          g=res.x[:n]
          t=res.x[n]
          if np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
             g=np.clip(g,0,S)
             return g,t
    except Exception:
       pass
    return None,np.inf
```
Upper t=10*S? If S~22, upper 222. If current bad with max conv huge (spike S^2 ~ S^2), t might need >222. But we start reasonable. For uniform score2. For random maybe cmax up to S? maybe <S^2. To be safe upper=10*S*S? That is 5120, LP scale. Use `10.0*max(S,1.0)+10.0` maybe. Since scores target ~1.5, t not large. If initial bad, LP may return t near high. Set upper=100*S? okay. But large upper may slow. Use `t_ub = 10.0*S + 10.0 * max(2.0, np.max(conv_square(a)))`? Could compute cmax? Expensive but we already have score maybe. We can approximate with `t_ub = max(10.0, 5.0*S, 2.0* (S*S/n)??)`. Simpler no upper: `(0.0, None)`. Highs can handle unbounded? Feasible bounded by constraints if a has positives; if not, t unbounded but a has sum S. Use no upper to avoid bounding issues. The LP objective min t, constraints A g - t <=0, g bounded 0,S; t implicit ≤ max row sum *S ≤ S*max(a)*? finite. So no upper needed.
Set bounds for t `(0.0, None)`.
A columns sum maybe each row sum = sum a; t ≤ S*max? finite.
If `a` has zeros, some g column constrained? Each column j appears in rows j..j+n-1 with coefficients a_{...}; if all those a zero? Is that possible? Column j coefficients are a[0..n-1] at rows j..j+n-1. If a all zero except maybe? Since sum S>0, for any j, if a_k positive for some k, column j has coefficient a_{j-k?} for row j+k? Specifically column j has a_i at row j+i. Since all i, if a positive anywhere, column j has positive at row j+i. So constrained. Good.

`linprog` may hit time limit; `res.x` maybe available. We can check `res.status == 1` optimal; status 0? Highs status codes: 0 optimal? In scipy linprog, `res.success` true if status 0. Time limit status? We'll just use success; if not, fallback.

Could use `methods='highs'` and `options={'time_limit': time_limit, 'presolve':'high', 'primal_feasibility_tolerance':1e-9, 'dual_feasibility_tolerance':1e-9}`. Some versions don't accept string presolve? Use `{'presolve': True, 'time_limit': float(time_limit)}`.

### LP refine loop with candidate blends
We need `rng` optional. For blends, if g is None break. Use cmax scores under sum S for speed:
```
def score_c_fixed(a): c=conv_square(a); return float(c.max()) # sum S
```
But after rescale sum S. If a sum slightly off due rounding, score_fast safer but slower. Use `score_fast` for blends? Blends few. Use `score_fast`.

```
def lp_refine(a_init,time_limit,rng=None,max_iters=50,lp_time=3.0,verbose=False):
    n=len(a_init); S=math.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a=rescale(a_init,S); best=a.copy(); best_score=score_fast(a)
    t0=time.monotonic(); stagn=0
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic()-t0 >= time_limit: break
       tleft=time_limit-(time.monotonic()-t0)
       g,tmin=lp_best_response(a,S,time_limit=min(lp_time, max(1.0,tleft/4)))
       if g is None: break
       g=rescale(g,S)
       # candidate list
       cand=None; cand_sc=best_score
       for eta in (0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0):
           x=(1.0-eta)*a + eta*g
           x=rescale(x,S)
           sc=score_fast(x)
           if sc < cand_sc:
               cand_sc=sc; cand=x.copy()
       # also evaluate g reversal? score invariant but maybe after rescale same.
       if cand is not None:
           a=cand; best=a.copy(); best_score=cand_sc; stagn=0
       else:
           stagn += 1
           if rng is not None:
              a=rescale(a + 0.02*rng.standard_normal(n), S)
           if stagn > 10: break
       if verbose and (it+1)%5==0: print(...)
    return best,best_score
```
Potential issue: LP from a with many zeros may produce g with zeros, blend may lose positivity. Softmax later clips floor. OK.

Time: LP time 3s, max 50 =>150s. Good.

### Gradient polish implementation with Adam and restart
Let's write robust:
```
def gradient_polish(a_init, time_limit, rng=None, tau_start=None, tau_end=None, base_lr=None, verbose=False):
    n=len(a_init); S=math.sqrt(2.0*n)
    if tau_start is None: tau_start = 10.0/n
    if tau_end is None: tau_end = 0.2/n
    if base_lr is None: base_lr = 0.35
    a0=rescale(a_init,S)
    theta=np.log(np.maximum(a0,1e-300)/S)
    theta=theta - float(np.max(theta))
    best_theta=theta.copy(); best_a=a0.copy(); best_score=score_fast(a0)
    m1=np.zeros(n); m2=np.zeros(n)
    beta1=0.9; beta2=0.999; eps=1e-8; step=0
    t0=time.monotonic(); lr=base_lr; orig_lr=base_lr; stall=0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
        elapsed=time.monotonic()-t0
        frac=min(1.0, elapsed/time_limit)
        tau=tau_start * (tau_end/tau_start)**frac
        a=softmax_logits(theta,S)
        c=conv_square(a)
        cmax=float(np.max(c))
        # exact score under sum S ~ cmax, but use score_fast maybe
        score=score_fast(a) # sum S
        # weights
        z=(c-cmax)/tau
        # clip z to [-100,0] to avoid underflow? exp(-1000) okay underflows to0; no issue but maybe overflow no z<=0.
        e=np.exp(z)
        e_sum=float(np.sum(e))
        if e_sum == 0 or not np.isfinite(e_sum): e=np.ones_like(c); e_sum=float(c.size)
        w=e/e_sum
        g=grad_from_weights(a,w)
        inner=float(np.dot(a,g))
        gt=a*g - (a/S)*inner
        # if gt all zero, break?
        step += 1
        m1 = beta1*m1 + (1-beta1)*gt
        m2 = beta2*m2 + (1-beta2)*(gt*gt)
        m1h = m1/(1.0-beta1**step)
        m2h = m2/(1.0-beta2**step)
        # adaptive lr with decay
        lr = orig_lr / math.sqrt(1.0 + 0.02*step)
        # maybe scale if values large: theta_step = lr * m1h/(sqrt(m2h)+eps)
        theta_new = theta - lr * m1h/(np.sqrt(m2h)+eps)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(theta_new)):
            theta = best_theta.copy(); m1*=0; m2*=0; lr*=0.5; stall=50; continue
        a_new=softmax_logits(theta_new,S)
        score_new=score_fast(a_new)
        if np.isfinite(score_new):
            if score_new < best_score:
                best_score=score_new; best_a=a_new.copy(); best_theta=theta_new.copy(); stall=0
            # accept if not much worse than current? We need current score variable.
        ...
```
Need accept/reject logic. Let's maintain `current_a`,`current_score`. Initialize current=best.
After new:
```
if np.isfinite(score_new):
    if score_new <= current_score + 0.0005: # accept small uphill
        theta=theta_new; current_a=a_new; current_score=score_new
        stall=0? if improved best reset, else maybe count
        if score_new >= best_score:
            stall +=1
    else:
        # try half step
        theta_half=theta - 0.5*lr*...
        a_half=...
        score_half=score_fast(a_half)
        if np.isfinite(score_half) and score_half <= current_score + 0.0005:
            theta=theta_half; current...; stall += 1
        else:
            # reject, decay lr
            lr = max(0.01, lr*0.5)
            stall +=1
else reject
# if stall > 100:
if stall > 100:
    theta=best_theta.copy() + (0.01*rng.standard_normal(n) if rng else 0)
    m1*=0; m2*=0; step=0; lr=orig_lr*0.7; stall=0
    current_a=softmax_logits(theta,S); current_score=score_fast(current_a)
```
But if no improvement for long, exact score stagnate. We can also accept based on smooth objective F even if exact increases. Let's compute F for current and new maybe. But extra. Maybe use small tolerance 0.002.

The soft objective may decrease while exact score increases; rejecting could hinder. Could accept all finite updates and only restart if exact score > best + 0.05 for many. Since we keep best, exact may wander. Let's do:
- Always accept finite `theta_new` (to follow smooth).

This allows exploration.

if bad_count>30: theta=best_theta+...; m reset; bad_count=0
```
At end return best_a.

This may sacrifice current but okay.

Need avoid `beta1**step` underflow for large step; step could 100k, `0.9**100000` underflows to 0, fine. But computing power each step maybe okay. Could update bias manually using `bias1=beta1*bias1+...`. Simpler:

### SA polish
Implement a simple one. Need compute contributions for max coefficient. Let's vectorize:
```
def make_peak_move(a, rng):
    n=len(a); c=conv_square(a); k=int(np.argmax(c))
    lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
    i=np.arange(lo,hi+1)
    j=k-i
    contrib=np.zeros(n)
    contrib[i]=a[i]*a[j]
    total=contrib.sum()
    # choose top min(8, len) contributions to decrease
    order=np.argsort(contrib)[::-1]
    top=order[:max(1,min(10,len(order)))]
    top_drop=rng.normal(-0.04,0.015,size=len(top))
    a[top]*=np.exp(top_drop)
    # choose some low contributors or random to increase
    rest=np.setdiff1d(np.arange(n), top)
    if len(rest)>0:
       inc_count=min(8,len(rest))
       inc=rng.choice(rest,size=inc_count,replace=False)
       a[inc]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0.04,0.015,size=inc_count))
    return a
```
Other moves:
- random segment multiplicative: width 2..max(3,n//8), start random, factor exp(normal(0,0.03,width)).
- global tiny noise.
Temperature: start `temp=0.001`; cooling 0.9995; if no improve 1000, reset temp? Score scale ~1.5, updates diff ~1e-4. Use `accept = rng.random() < np.exp(-(new_score-current_score)/temp)`. If new_score - current_score large, not.
Need current tracking and best.
```
def sa_polish(a_init,time_limit,rng):
   n; S; a=rescale; current_score=score_fast(a); best...
   temp=0.0008; cool=0.9995; t0=time.monotonic(); iter=0
   while time:
      cand=a.copy(); r=rng.random()
      if r<0.4: cand=make_peak_move(cand,rng)
      elif r<0.7: segment
      elif r<0.85: two segments
      else: cand*=exp(rng.normal(0,0.005,n))
      cand=np.clip(cand,0,None); cand=rescale(cand,S)
      new_score=score_fast(cand)
      if new_score <= current_score: accept; if <best update
      elif rng.random() < math.exp(-min(50,(new_score-current_score)/temp)): accept
      temp*=cool
      if temp < 1e-6: temp=1e-5 (or break)
   return best
```
Using `math.exp` with positive? new_score-current_score positive. If temp small, exponent very negative; cap to avoid underflow.

### Optimization allocation and candidate generation
Let's define `generate_candidates(provided, rng)`:
```
def resample_seq(a,n):
    if len(a)==n: return a.copy()
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,len(a)); x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    return np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
```
If provided has many zeros, interp okay.
Inits:
```
def make_inits(n,rng,provided):
    inits=[]
    S=math.sqrt(2*n)
    if provided is not None and len(provided)>1:
       inits.append(('provided', resample_seq(provided,n)))
    inits.append(('uniform', np.ones(n)))
    # bathtub
    for s in [0.5,1.0,2.0,3.0]:
       i=np.arange(n); center=(n-1)/2.0; d=np.abs(i-center)/max(1,n/2.0)
       a=np.exp(s*(0.5-d)) # edges 1+s*0.5, center 1
       a *= 1.0+0.05*rng.standard_normal(n)
       inits.append(('bat%d'%round(s), a))
    # chirp
    for alpha in [0.3,0.7,1.2]:
       t=np.arange(n)
       a=1.0+alpha*np.cos(2*np.pi*t*t/n + rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
       a += 0.05*rng.standard_normal(n); inits.append(('chirp',np.maximum(a,0.01)))
    # random smooth
    for _ in range(2):
       a=1.0+0.3*rng.standard_normal(n)
       # smooth via cumulative? Use FFT random phases
       f=np.fft.rfft(a); f *= 1.0/(1.0+np.fft.rfftfreq(n)* (n/10.0)) # lowpass?
       a=np.real(np.fft.irfft(f,n)); a=1.0+0.3*a; inits.append(('smooth',np.maximum(a,0.01)))
    # sparse high
    k=max(8,int(0.15*n))
    pos=rng.choice(n,size=k,replace=False)
    a=np.full(n,0.02)
    a[pos]=1.0
    inits.append(('sparse',a))
    # sparse with random weights
    a=np.full(n,0.02); weights=0.5+rng.random(k); a[pos? need pos again]
```

`fft_convolve` generic:
```
def fft_convolve(a, b):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    need = len(a) + len(b) - 1
    if need <= 0: return np.array([])
    size = 1
    while size < need: size <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    fb = np.fft.rfft(b, size)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fb, size)
    return out[:need]
```
For square, use `conv_square` for speed.

`conv_square`:
```
def conv_square(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=len(a)
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    need=2*n-1
    size=1
    while size < need: size <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,size)
    out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,size)
    return out[:need]
```

`score_fast` clips. For optimization, use `score_sumS`:
```
def score_sumS(a):
    c=conv_square(a)
    return float(np.max(c)) if c.size else np.inf
```
No sum check because sum S.

`rescale`:
```
def rescale(a,S):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return a.copy()
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-300:
       return np.full(a.shape, S/max(1,a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a*(S/s)
```

`softmax_logits`:
```
def softmax_logits(theta,S):
    theta=np.asarray(theta,dtype=np.float64)
    z=theta - float(np.max(theta))
    e=np.exp(z)
    s=float(np.sum(e))
    if s <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(s):
       return np.full(theta.shape, S/max(1,theta.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return e*(S/s)
```

`grad_from_weights`:
```
def grad_from_weights(a,w):
    n=len(a)
    if n==0: return a.copy()
    conv=fft_convolve(a[::-1], w)
    return 2.0*conv[n-1:n-1+n]
```
Check slice length n? `n-1+n = 2n-1`, start n-1, end exclusive 2n-1, length n. For n=1, start0 end1 length1.

`build_cross_matrix` maybe inside lp? top-level:
```
def build_cross_matrix(a):
   n=len(a); A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n)); 
   for j in range(n): A[j:j+n,j]=a
   return A
```
But LP adds t column. We'll build full in LP.

`lp_best_response`:
```
def lp_best_response(a,S,time_limit=5.0):
   n=len(a)
   if n==0: return None, np.inf
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
   rows=2*n-1
   A=np.zeros((rows,n+1), dtype=np.float64)
   A[:,n] = -1.0
   for j in range(n):
       A[j:j+n,j] = a
   c=np.zeros(n+1); c[n]=1.0
   b_ub=np.zeros(rows)
   A_eq=np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1.0
   b_eq=np.array([S], dtype=np.float64)
   bounds=[(0.0,S)]*n
   bounds.append((0.0,None))
   try:
      res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve': True, 'time_limit': float(max(0.1,time_limit))})
      if res.success:
         g=np.asarray(res.x[:n], dtype=np.float64)
         t=float(res.x[n])
         if np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
            g=np.clip(g,0.0,S)
            return g,t
   except Exception:
      pass
   return None, np.inf
```
If LP fails but has feasible? ignore.
Note: `bounds=[(0.0,S)]*n` lists tuple immutable okay.

`lp_refine` as above. Use `score_sumS` for blends.
```
def lp_refine(a_init,time_limit,rng=None,max_iters=40,lp_time=2.5,verbose=False):
    n=len(a_init); S=math.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a=rescale(a_init,S); best=a.copy(); best_score=score_sumS(a)
    t0=time.monotonic(); stagn=0; it=0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit and it<max_iters:
       tleft=time_limit-(time.monotonic()-t0)
       if tleft < 2.0: break
       g,tmin=lp_best_response(a,S,time_limit=min(lp_time, max(1.0, tleft*0.25)))
       it+=1
       if g is None: break
       g=rescale(g,S)
       cand=None; cand_sc=best_score
       for eta in (0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0):
          x=(1.0-eta)*a + eta*g
          x=rescale(x,S)
          sc=score_sumS(x)
          if sc < cand_sc:
             cand_sc=sc; cand=x.copy()
       if cand is not None:
          a=cand; best=a.copy(); best_score=cand_sc; stagn=0
       else:
          stagn +=1
          if rng is not None:
             a=rescale(a + 0.02*rng.standard_normal(n), S)
          if stagn > 12: break
       if verbose and it%5==0: print(...)
    return best,best_score
```
If `a` perturbed after no improvement, might be bad; okay.
Could include reverse of a? not.

`gradient_adam`:
```
def gradient_adam(a_init, time_limit, rng=None, mode='softmax', base_lr=None, tau_start=None, tau_end=None, verbose=False):
    n=len(a_init); S=math.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a0=rescale(a_init,S)
    maxa=float(np.max(a0)) if a0.size else 1.0
    floor=max(1e-12*max(maxa,1e-6), 1e-300)
    a_safe=np.maximum(a0, floor)
    theta=np.log(a_safe/S)
    theta=theta - float(np.max(theta))
    best_a=a_safe.copy(); best_score=score_sumS(best_a)
    theta=theta.copy(); best_theta=theta.copy()
    m1=np.zeros(n); m2=np.zeros(n)
    bias1=1.0; bias2=1.0 # but bias formula: 1-beta1^step. Start 1? For step0? Let's do standard: m update, bias1 = beta1*bias1 + (1-beta1) with bias1=1? Initially after first update bias1=1? Better initialize bias1=1.0? Then m1h=m1/(1-beta1^step). If step=1, denominator=1-beta1. Initialize step=0, bias1=1.0; after update bias1 += (1- beta1)*(1 - bias1)? Simpler use step count and `bias1 = 1.0 - beta1**step` each step, but power costly. Use recurrence: start bias1=0.0; after m1 update: bias1 = beta1*bias1 + (1-beta1). For step1, bias1=0.1. good. bias2 similarly.
    beta1=0.9; beta2=0.999; eps=1e-8; step=0
    if base_lr is None: base_lr = 0.25 if mode=='softmax' else 0.08
    orig_lr=base_lr; lr=base_lr
    # compute tau initial if softmax
    if mode=='softmax' and tau_start is None:
       c0=conv_square(a_safe); cmax0=float(np.max(c0)); spread=max(0.05, cmax0 - float(np.mean(c0)))
       tau_start=0.25*spread
       tau_end=max(0.001, 0.005*spread)
    if mode=='peak':
       tol_start=0.05; tol_end=0.0005; top_k=8
    t0=time.monotonic(); bad=0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < time_limit:
       elapsed=time.monotonic()-t0; frac=min(1.0, elapsed/time_limit)
       a=softmax_logits(theta,S)
       c=conv_square(a)
       cmax=float(np.max(c))
       if mode=='softmax':
          tau=tau_start * (tau_end/tau_start)**frac
          z=(c-cmax)/tau
          # z <=0 but due rounding maybe >0 small; clip
          z=np.minimum(z,0.0)
          e=np.exp(z)
          esum=float(np.sum(e))
          if esum <=0 or not math.isfinite(esum): e=np.ones_like(c); esum=float(c.size)
          w=e/esum
       else:
          tol=tol_start * (tol_end/tol_start)**frac
          # active by top and within tol
          cmax_local=cmax
          active = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax_local - tol)
          if active.size == 0:
             active = np.argsort(c)[-top_k:]
          elif active.size > 32:
             # order active by value, take top 32
             order = np.argsort(c[active])[::-1][:32]
             active = active[order]
          w=np.zeros(c.shape, dtype=np.float64)
          w[active]=1.0/float(len(active))
       g=grad_from_weights(a,w)
       inner=float(np.dot(a,g))
       gt=a*g - (a/S)*inner
       # If gt variance zero, perturb theta
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(gt)):
          theta=best_theta.copy(); m1.fill(0); m2.fill(0); bias1=0; bias2=0; step=0; bad=50; continue
       step +=1
       m1 = beta1*m1 + (1-beta1)*gt
       m2 = beta2*m2 + (1-beta2)*(gt*gt)
       bias1 = beta1*bias1 + (1-beta1)
       bias2 = beta2*bias2 + (1-beta2)
       m1h=m1/bias1
       m2h=m2/bias2
       lr = orig_lr / math.sqrt(1.0 + 0.02*step)
       delta = lr * m1h/(np.sqrt(m2h)+eps)
       theta_new = theta - delta
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(theta_new)):
          theta=best_theta.copy(); m1.fill(0); m2.fill(0); bias1=0; bias2=0; step=0; bad=50; continue
       theta=theta_new
       a_new=softmax_logits(theta,S)
       score_new=float(np.max(conv_square(a_new)))
       if np.isfinite(score_new):
          if score_new < best_score:
             best_score=score_new; best_a=a_new.copy(); best_theta=theta.copy()
             bad=0
          if score_new > best_score + 0.02:
             bad +=1
          else:
             bad = max(0, bad-1)
          if bad > 60:
             if rng is not None:
                theta = best_theta + 0.01*rng.standard_normal(n)
             else:
                theta = best_theta.copy()
             m1.fill(0); m2.fill(0); bias1=0; bias2=0; step=0
             bad=0
       if verbose and step%1000==0:
          print(f"    grad mode={mode} step={step} score={score_new:.6f} best={best_score:.6f}")
    return best_a, best_score
```
Potential issue: `score_new` may be referenced before assignment in verbose if not finite. Put default.

In peak mode, active `np.argsort(c[active])` if active large; okay.

`sa_polish`:
```
def make_peak_move(a,rng):
   n=len(a); c=conv_square(a); k=int(np.argmax(c))
   lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
   if hi < lo: return a
   i=np.arange(lo,hi+1); j=k-i
   contrib=np.zeros(n)
   contrib[i]=a[i]*a[j]
   order=np.argsort(contrib)[::-1]
   top=order[:max(1,min(8,n))]
   a=top.copy? We modify passed copy. 
   a[top]*=np.exp(rng.normal(-0.04,0.012,size=top.size))
   rest=np.setdiff1d(np.arange(n), top)
   if rest.size>0:
      inc_n=min(8,rest.size)
      inc=rng.choice(rest,size=inc_n,replace=False)
      a[inc]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0.04,0.012,size=inc_n))
   return a
```
But `np.setdiff1d` sorted, okay. If top size n, rest empty.
Segment move:

def make_segment_move(a,rng):
   n=len(a); width=int(rng.integers(1, max(2,n//8)))
   start=int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1))
   a[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.025,size=width))
   return a
```

`optimize_candidate`:
```
def optimize_candidate(a_init,time_limit,rng=None,do_lp=True,verbose=False):
   n=len(a_init); best=a_init.copy(); best_score=score_fast(a_init)
   t0=time.monotonic()
   if do_lp and 32 <= n <= 768:
      lp_budget=min(0.35*time_limit, 150.0)
      rem=time_limit-(time.monotonic()-t0)
      if rem > lp_budget and lp_budget > 10:
          a2,sc2=lp_refine(a_init, lp_budget, rng=rng, max_iters=25, lp_time=2.0, verbose=verbose)
          if sc2 < best_score:
              best=a2; best_score=sc2
   t1=time.monotonic(); rem=time_limit-(t1-t0)
   # gradient budget
   if rem > 20:
      grad_budget=min(0.55*time_limit, 350.0, rem*0.6)
      if grad_budget > 10:
         a2,sc2=gradient_adam(best, grad_budget, rng=rng, mode='softmax', base_lr=0.25, verbose=verbose)
         if sc2 < best_score: best=a2; best_score=sc2
   t2=time.monotonic(); rem=time_limit-(t2-t0)
   if rem > 20:
      peak_budget=min(0.25*time_limit, 150.0, rem*0.7)
      if peak_budget > 10:
         a2,sc2=gradient_adam(best, peak_budget, rng=rng, mode='peak', base_lr=0.08, verbose=verbose)
         if sc2 < best_score: best=a2; best_score=sc2
   # SA if rem
   t3=time.monotonic(); rem=time_limit-(t3-t0)
   if rem > 20 and rng is not None:
      sa_budget=min(0.2*time_limit, 120.0, rem*0.8)
      if sa_budget > 10:
         a2,sc2=sa_polish(best, sa_budget, rng)
         if sc2 < best_score: best=a2; best_score=sc2
   # final local exact
   final_score=score_fast(best)
   if final_score < best_score: best_score=final_score
   return best, best_score
```

`candidate_from_seq`:
```
def make_candidate_from_raw(raw, n):
    a=np.asarray(raw,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    if a.size != n:
       if a.size ==0: a=np.ones(n)
       else: a=resample_seq(a,n)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    return a
```

`resample_seq`:
```
def resample_seq(a,n):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
   if a.size == n: return a.copy()
   if a.size ==0: return np.ones(n)
   x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,a.size)
   x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   return np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
```

`make_inits` detailed:
```
def make_inits(n, rng, provided=None):
    inits=[]
    if provided is not None and len(provided)>0:
       inits.append(resample_seq(provided,n).copy())
    inits.append(np.ones(n))
    # bathtub
    i=np.arange(n,float)
    center=(n-1)/2.0
    d=np.abs(i-center)/(n-1 if n>1 else 1.0)
    svals=[0.5,1.0,2.0]
    for idx,s in enumerate(svals):
       a=np.exp(s*(0.5 - d) + 0.02*rng.standard_normal(n))
       inits.append(np.maximum(a,1e-6))
    # Fourier even
    x=i/(n-1 if n>1 else 1.0)
    params=[(2,0.5),(2,1.0),(4,0.7),(6,0.5)]
    for k,amp in params:
       phase=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
       a=1.0 + amp*math.cos(2*np.pi*k*x + phase) # edges? phase random; okay
       a += 0.03*rng.standard_normal(n)
       inits.append(np.maximum(a,1e-6))
    # random smooth
    for _ in range(2):
       a=1.0+0.4*rng.standard_normal(n)
       f=np.fft.rfft(a)
       freq=np.fft.rfftfreq(n)
       f = f/(1.0+5.0*freq)
       a=np.real(np.fft.irfft(f,n))
       a=1.0+0.3*a
       inits.append(np.maximum(a,1e-6))
    # sparse edge
    for trial in range(2):
       a=np.full(n,0.01)
       k=int(rng.integers(max(8, int(math.sqrt(n)*0.8)), max(9, int(math.sqrt(n)*2.5)+1)))
       u=rng.beta(1.5,1.5,k)
       pos=np.unique((u*n).astype(int))
       pos=pos[(pos>=0)&(pos<n)]
       if pos.size>0:
          w=rng.uniform(0.3,2.0,pos.size)
          a[pos]+=w
       inits.append(a)
    return inits
```
Need avoid duplicate arrays? okay.
For n small (e.g. 64), ranges okay.

In propose, for each init, `a=rescale(init,S); sc=score_sumS(a); if finite add pool`.
Could evaluate provided true early and add.

def propose_candidate(seed=42,budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    if budget_s is None: budget_s=1800
    t_global=time.monotonic(); deadline=t_global+float(budget_s)-30.0
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    provided=None
    if 'height_sequence_1' in globals():
       raw=globals()['height_sequence_1']
       if raw is not None:
          arr=np.asarray(raw,dtype=np.float64)
          arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
          if arr.size>0: provided=arr
    pool=[]
    # add provided original
    if provided is not None:
       s_prov=score_fast(provided)
       if np.isfinite(s_prov): pool.append((s_prov,provided.copy()))
       # true if available?
    # uniform 256
    unif=np.ones(256)
    pool.append((score_fast(unif),unif))
    # generate candidates
    base_n = len(provided) if provided is not None else 256
    length_set=set()
    for L in [base_n, max(64, base_n//2), min(2048, 2*base_n), 256,384,512,768,1024]:
       if 32 <= L <= 2048: length_set.add(int(L))
    # maybe if base_n large, add 1536? 
    if base_n > 512: length_set.add(min(2048, max(32, base_n)))
    lengths=sorted(length_set)
    for n in lengths:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 120: break
       S=math.sqrt(2.0*n)
       inits=make_inits(n,rng,provided)
       # evaluate local
       for init in inits:
          if time.monotonic() > deadline - 120: break
          a=rescale(init,S)
          if a.size==0: continue
          sc=score_sumS(a)
          if np.isfinite(sc):
              pool.append((sc,a.copy()))
       if len(pool)>50:
          pool.sort(key=pool_key)
          pool=pool[:30]
    if not pool:
       pool=[(2.0,np.ones(256))]
    pool.sort(key=pool_key)
    top=pool[:8]
    best_seq=None; best_score=np.inf
    for sc,seq in top[:2]:
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
           best_score=sc; best_seq=seq.copy()
    # quick refine top candidates
    alloc_list=[240,180,120,80,50,30,20,20]
    for idx,(sc,seq) in enumerate(top):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 120: break
       alloc = alloc_list[idx] if idx < len(alloc_list) else 20
       alloc = min(float(alloc), max(10.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 120))
       if alloc < 15: break
       n=len(seq)
       do_lp = (n <= 768)
       # if initial score much worse than current best, shorter
       if sc > best_score + 0.05:
           alloc = min(alloc, 40.0)
       cand, score = optimize_candidate(seq, alloc, rng=rng, do_lp=do_lp, verbose=False)
       if score < best_score:
          best_score=score; best_seq=cand.copy()
       pool2=(score,cand.copy()); pool.append(pool2); pool.sort(key=pool_key); pool=pool[:20]
    # final heavy polish on best_seq
    if best_seq is None:
       best_seq=np.ones(256); best_score=2.0
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 20:
       final_budget = min(600.0, remaining*0.9)
       # if best n <=768, allow LP short; else no
       cand, score = optimize_candidate(best_seq, final_budget, rng=rng, do_lp=(len(best_seq) <= 768), verbose=True)
       if score < best_score:
          best_score=score; best_seq=cand.copy()
    # final true selection from top pool
    pool.sort(key=pool_key)
    result_list=None; result_score=np.inf
    # include best_seq
    uniq_pool=[]
    seen_lengths_scores=[]
    for sc,seq in pool[:6]:
       lst=[float(x) for x in np.asarray(seq,dtype=np.float64)]
       ts = try_true(lst)
       uniq_pool.append((ts,sc,lst))
    # also best_seq
    lst_best=[float(x) for x in best_seq]
    ts_best=try_true(lst_best)
    uniq_pool.append((ts_best,best_score,lst_best))
    # uniform fallback
    if 'evaluate_sequence' in globals():
        try:
            ts_unif=evaluate_sequence([1.0]*256)
        except Exception: ts_unif=np.inf
        uniq_pool.append((ts_unif,2.0,[1.0]*256))
    else:
        uniq_pool.append((np.inf,2.0,[1.0]*256))
    uniq_pool.sort(key=lambda? cannot lambda. Need top-level pool_key2 returns item[0].)
```
